LG1867	1234	Not a mutineer walks handcuff'd to jail, but I am handcuff'd to him and walk by his side;
LG1867	1235	(I am less the jolly one there, and more the silent one,with sweat on my twitching lips.)
LG1867	1236	Not a youngster is taken for larceny, but I go up too,and am tried and sentenced.

LG1867	1241	Enough! enough! enough!
LG1867	1242	Somehow I have been stunn'd. Stand back!
LG1867	1243	Give me a little time beyond my cuff'd head, slumbers,dreams, gaping;

LG1867	1245	That I could forget the mockers and insults!

LG1867	1249	I resume the overstaid fraction;
LG1867	1250	The grave of rock multiplies what has been confided to it, or to any graves;
LG1867	1251	Corpses rise, gashes heal, fastenings roll from me.

LG1867	1257	Continue your annotations, continue your question-ingsquestionings.
LG1867	1258	The friendly and flowing savage, Who is he?

LG1867	1263	They desire he should like them, touch them, speak to them, stay with them.

LG1867	1266	They descend in new forms from the tips of his fingers;
LG1867	1267	They are wafted with the odor of his body or breath —they fly out of the glance of his eyes.
LG1867	1268	Flaunt of the sunshine, I need not your bask,—lie over!
LG1867	1269	You light surface only—I force surfaces and depths also.
LG1867	1270	Earth! you seem to look for something at my hands;
LG1867	1271	Say, old Top-knot! what do you want?
LG1867	1272	Man or woman! I might tell how I like you, but cannot;
LG1867	1273	And might tell what it is in me, and what it is in you,but cannot;
LG1867	1274	And might tell that pining I have—that pulse of my nights and days.
LG1867	1275	Behold! I do not give lectures or a little charity;
LG1867	1276	What I give, I give out of myself.
LG1867	1277	You there, impotent, loose in the knees!
LG1867	1278	Open your scarf'd chops till I blow grit within you;
LG1867	1279	Spread your palms, and lift the flaps of your pockets;
LG1867	1280	I am not to be denied—I compel—I have stores plenty and to spare;
LG1867	1281	And anything I have I bestow.
LG1867	1282	I do not ask who you are—that is not so important to me;
LG1867	1283	You can do nothing, and be nothing, but what I will infold you.
LG1867	1284	To cotton-field drudge or cleaner of privies I lean;
LG1867	1285	On his right cheek I put the family kiss,
LG1867	1286	And deep in my soul I swear, I never will deny him.
LG1867	1287	On women fit for conception I start bigger and nim-blernimbler babes;
LG1867	1288	This day I am jetting the stuff of far more arrogant republics.
LG1867	1289	To any one dying—thither I speed, and twist the knob of the door;
LG1867	1290	Turn the bed-clothes toward the foot of the bed;
LG1867	1291	Let the physician and the priest go home.
LG1867	1292	I seize the descending man, and raise him with re-sistlessresistless will.
LG1867	1293	O despairer, here is my neck;
LG1867	1294	By God! you shall not go down! Hang your whole weight upon me.
LG1867	1295	I dilate you with tremendous breath—I buoy you up;
LG1867	1296	Every room of the house do I fill with an arm'd force,
LG1867	1297	Lovers of me, bafflers of graves.
LG1867	1298	Sleep! I and they keep guard all night;
LG1867	1299	Not doubt—not decease shall dare to lay finger upon you;
LG1867	1300	I have embraced you, and henceforth possess you to myself; And when you rise in the morning you will find what I tell you is so.
LG1867	1301	I am he bringing help for the sick as they pant on their backs;
LG1867	1302	And for strong upright men I bring yet more needed help.
LG1867	1303	I heard what was said of the universe;
LG1867	1304	Heard it and heard it of several thousand years:
LG1867	1305	It is middling well as far as it goes,—But is that all?
LG1867	1306	Magnifying and applying come I,
LG1867	1307	Outbidding at the start the old cautious hucksters,
LG1867	1308	Taking myself the exact dimensions of Jehovah,

LG1867	1949	It has done its work—I toss it carelessly to fall where it may.
LG1867	1950	ONE hour to madness and joy!
LG1867	1951	O furious! O confine me not!
LG1867	1952	(What is this that frees me so in storms?
LG1867	1953	What do my shouts amid lightnings and raging winds mean?)
LG1867	1954	O to drink the mystic deliria deeper than any other man!
LG1867	1955	O savage and tender achings!
LG1867	1956	(I bequeath them to you, my children,
LG1867	1957	I tell them to you, for reasons, O bridegroom and bride.)
LG1867	1958	O to be yielded to you, whoever you are, and you to be yielded to me, in defiance of the world!
LG1867	1959	O to return to Paradise! O bashful and feminine!
LG1867	1960	O to draw you to me—to plant on you for the first time the lips of a determin'd man!
LG1867	1961	O the puzzle—the thrice-tied knot—the deep and dark pool! O all untied and illumin'd!
LG1867	1962	O to speed where there is space enough and air enough at last!
LG1867	1963	O to be absolv'd from previous ties and conventions— I from mine, and you from yours!
LG1867	1964	O to find a new unthought-of nonchalance with the best of nature!

LG1867	1969	O to escape utterly from others' anchors and holds!

LG1867	1974	To be lost, if it must be so!
LG1867	1975	To feed the remainder of life with one hour of fulness and freedom!

LG1867	1977	WE two—how long we were fool'd!
LG1867	1978	Now transmuted, we swiftly escape, as Nature escapes;
LG1867	1979	We are Nature—long have we been absent, but now we return;
LG1867	1980	We become plants, leaves, foliage, roots, bark;
LG1867	1981	We are bedded in the ground—we are rocks;
LG1867	1982	We are oaks—we grow in the openings side by side;

LG1867	1985	We are what the locust blossoms are—we drop scent around the lanes, mornings and evenings;
LG1867	1986	We are also the coarse smut of beasts, vegetables, minerals;
LG1867	1987	We are two predatory hawks—we soar above, and look down;

LG1867	1995	We have voided all but freedom, and all but our own joy.
LG1867	1996	NATIVE moments! when you come upon me—Ah you are here now!

LG1867	2016	FACING west, from California's shores,

LG1867	2048	WHO has gone farthest? For I swear I will go farther;
LG1867	2049	And who has been just? For I would be the most just person of the earth;
LG1867	2050	And who most cautious? For I would be more cautious;
LG1867	2051	And who has been happiest? O I think it is I! I think no one was ever happier than I;
LG1867	2052	And who has lavish'd all? For I lavish constantly the best I have;
LG1867	2053	And who has been firmest? For I would be firmer;

LG1867	2063	IN paths untrodden,
LG1867	2064	In the growth by margins of pond-waters,

LG1867	2081	SCENTED herbage of my breast,

LG1867	2159	THESE, I, singing in spring, collect for lovers,

LG1867	2729	(You will come forward in due time to my side.)
LG1867	2730	My spirit has pass'd in compassion and determina-tiondetermination around the whole earth;

LG1867	2734	I think I have blown with you, O winds;

LG1867	2739	What cities the light or warmth penetrates, I pen-etratepenetrate those cities myself;
LG1867	2740	All islands to which birds wing their way, I wing my way myself.
LG1867	2741	Toward all,
LG1867	2742	I raise high the perpendicular hand—I make the signal,
LG1867	2743	To remain after me in sight forever,
LG1867	2744	For all the haunts and homes of men.
LG1867	2745	WHAT place is besieged, and vainly tries to raise the siege?
LG1867	2746	Lo! I send to that place a commander, swift, brave, immortal;
LG1867	2747	And with him horse and foot—and parks of artillery,
LG1867	2748	And artillerymen, the deadliest that ever fired gun.
LG1867	2749	THERE was a child went forth every day;
LG1867	2750	And the first object he look'd upon, that object he be-camebecame;
LG1867	2751	And that object became part of him for the day, or a certain part of the day, or for many years, or stretching cycles of years.
LG1867	2752	The early lilacs became part of this child,
LG1867	2753	And grass, and white and red morning-glories, and white and red clover, and the song of the phoebe-birdphoebe-bird,
LG1867	2754	And the Third-month lambs, and the sow's pink-faint litter, and the mare's foal, and the cow's calf,
LG1867	2755	And the noisy brood of the barn-yard, or by the mire of the pond-side,
LG1867	2756	And the fish suspending themselves so curiously below there—and the beautiful curious liquid,
LG1867	2757	And the water-plants with their graceful flat heads— all became part of him.
LG1867	2758	The field-sprouts of Fourth-month and Fifth-month became part of him;
LG1867	2759	Winter-grain sprouts, and those of the light-yellow corn, and the esculent roots of the garden,
LG1867	2760	And the apple-trees cover'd with blossoms, and the fruit afterward, and wood-berries, and the com-monestcommonest weeds by the road;
LG1867	2761	And the old drunkard staggering home from the out-houseouthouse of the tavern, whence he had lately risen,

LG1867	2763	And the friendly boys that pass'd—and the quarrel-somequarrelsome boys,

LG1867	2765	And all the changes of city and country, wherever he went.
LG1867	2766	His own parents;
LG1867	2767	He that had father'd him, and she that had conceiv'd him in her womb, and birth'd him,
LG1867	2768	They gave this child more of themselves than that;
LG1867	2769	They gave him afterward every day—they became part of him.
LG1867	2770	The mother at home, quietly placing the dishes on the supper-table;
LG1867	2771	The mother with mild words—clean her cap and gown.a wholesome odor falling off her person and clothes as she walks by;
LG1867	2772	The father, strong, self-sufficient, manly, mean, anger'd.unjust;
LG1867	2773	The blow, the quick loud word, the tight bargain, the crafty lure,
LG1867	2774	The family usages, the language, the company, the fur-niturefurniture—the yearning and swelling heart,
LG1867	2775	Affection that will not be gainsay'd—the sense of what is real—the thought if, after all, it should prove unreal,
LG1867	2776	The doubts of day-time and the doubts of night-time —the curious whether and how,
LG1867	2777	Whether that which appears so is so, or is it all flashes and specks?
LG1867	2778	Men and women crowding fast in the streets—if they are not flashes and specks, what are they?
LG1867	2779	The streets themselves, and the façades of houses, and goods in the windows,
LG1867	2780	Vehicles, teams, the heavy-plank'd wharves—the huge crossing at the ferries,

LG1867	2786	The horizon's edge, the flying sea-crow, the fragrance of salt-marsh and shore-mud;
LG1867	2787	These became part of that child who went forth every day, and who now goes, and will always go forth every day.
LG1867	2788	MYSELF and mine gymnastic ever,
LG1867	2789	To stand the cold or heat—to take good aim with a gun—to sail a boat—to manage horses—to be-getbeget superb children,
LG1867	2790	To speak readily and clearly—to feel at home among common people,
LG1867	2791	And to hold our own in terrible positions, on land and sea.
LG1867	2792	Not for an embroiderer;
LG1867	2793	(There will always be plenty of embroiderers—I wel-comewelcome them also;)
LG1867	2794	But for the fibre of things, and for inherent men and women.
LG1867	2795	Not to chisel ornaments,
LG1867	2796	But to chisel with free stroke the heads and limbs of plenteous Supreme Gods, that The States may realize them, walking and talking.
LG1867	2797	Let me have my own way;
LG1867	2798	Let others promulge the laws—I will make no account of the laws;
LG1867	2799	Let others praise eminent men and hold up peace— I hold up agitation and conflict;
LG1867	2800	I praise no eminent man—I rebuke to his face the one that was thought most worthy.
LG1867	2801	(Who are you? you mean devil! And what are you secretly guilty of, all your life?
LG1867	2802	Will you turn aside all your life? Will you grub and chatter all your life?)
LG1867	2803	(And who are you—blabbing by rote, years, pages,languages, reminiscences,
LG1867	2804	Unwitting to-day that you do not know how to speak a single word?)
LG1867	2805	Let others finish specimens—I never finish speci-mensspecimens;
LG1867	2806	I shower them by exhaustless laws, as nature does,fresh and modern continually.
LG1867	2807	I give nothing as duties;
LG1867	2808	What others give as duties, I give as living impulses;
LG1867	2809	(Shall I give the heart's action as a duty?)
LG1867	2810	Let others dispose of questions—I dispose of noth-ingnothing—I arouse unanswerable questions;
LG1867	2811	Who are they I see and touch, and what about them?
LG1867	2812	What about these likes of myself, that draw me so close by tender directions and indirections?
LG1867	2813	I call to the world to distrust the accounts of my friends, but listen to my enemies—as I myself do;
LG1867	2814	I charge you, too, forever, reject those who would ex-poundexpound me—for I cannot expound myself;
LG1867	2815	I charge that there be no theory or school founded out of me;
LG1867	2816	I charge you to leave all free, as I have left all free.
LG1867	2817	After me, vista!
LG1867	2818	O, I see life is not short, but immeasurably long;
LG1867	2819	I henceforth tread the world, chaste, temperate, an early riser, a steady grower,
LG1867	2820	Every hour the semen of centuries—and still of cen-turiescenturies.
LG1867	2821	I will follow up these continual lessons of the air,water, earth;
LG1867	2822	I perceive I have no time to lose.
LG1867	2823	WHO learns my lesson complete?
LG1867	2824	Boss, journeyman, apprentice—churchman and athe-istatheist,
LG1867	2825	The stupid and the wise thinker—parents and off-springoffspring—merchant, clerk, porter, and customer,
LG1867	2826	Editor, author, artist, and schoolboy—Draw nigh and commence;
LG1867	2827	It is no lesson—it lets down the bars to a good lesson,
LG1867	2828	And that to another, and every one to another still.
LG1867	2829	The great laws take and effuse without argument;
LG1867	2830	I am of the same style, for I am their friend,
LG1867	2831	I love them quits and quits—I do not halt and make salaams.
LG1867	2832	I lie abstracted, and hear beautiful tales of things,and the reasons of things
LG1867	2833	They are so beautiful, I nudge myself to listen.
LG1867	2834	I cannot say to any person what I hear—I cannot say it to myself—it is very wonderful.
LG1867	2835	It is no small matter, this round and delicious globe.moving so exactly in its orbit forever and ever,without one jolt, or the untruth of a single second;
LG1867	2836	I do not think it was made in six days, nor in ten thousand years, nor ten billions of years,
LG1867	2837	Nor plann'd and built one thing after another, as an architect plans and builds a house.
LG1867	2838	I do not think seventy years is the time of a man or woman,
LG1867	2839	Nor that seventy millions of years is the time of a man or woman,
LG1867	2840	Nor that years will ever stop the existence of me, or any one else.
LG1867	2841	Is it wonderful that I should be immortal? as every one is immortal;
LG1867	2842	I know it is wonderful—but my eye-sight is equally wonderful, and how I was conceived in my mother's womb is equally wonderful;
LG1867	2843	And pass'd from a babe, in the creeping trance of a couple of summers and winters, to articulate and walk—All this is equally wonderful.
LG1867	2844	And that my Soul embraces you this hour, and we affect each other without ever seeing each other,and never perhaps to see each other, is every bit as wonderful.
LG1867	2845	And that I can think such thoughts as these, is just as wonderful;
LG1867	2846	And that I can remind you, and you think them and know them to be true, is just as wonderful.
LG1867	2847	And that the moon spins round the earth, and on with the earth, is equally wonderful;
LG1867	2848	And that they balance themselves with the sun and stars, is equally wonderful.
LG1867	2849	WHOEVER you are, I fear you are walking the walks of dreams,
LG1867	2850	I fear those supposed realities are to melt from under your feet and hands;
LG1867	2851	Even now, your features, joys, speech, house, trade,manners, troubles, follies, costume, crimes,dissipate away from you,
LG1867	2852	Your true Soul and Body appear before me,
LG1867	2853	They stand forth out of affairs—out of commerce,shops, law, science, work, farms, clothes, the house, medicine, print, buying, selling, eating,drinking, suffering, dying.
LG1867	2854	Whoever you are, now I place my hand upon you,that you be my poem;
LG1867	2855	I whisper with my lips close to your ear,
LG1867	2856	I have loved many women and men, but I love none better than you.
LG1867	2857	O I have been dilatory and dumb;
LG1867	2858	I should have made my way straight to you long ago;
LG1867	2859	I should have blabb'd nothing but you, I should have chanted nothing but you.
LG1867	2860	I will leave all, and come and make the hymns of you;
LG1867	2861	None have understood you, but I understand you;
LG1867	2862	None have done justice to you—you have not done justice to yourself;
LG1867	2863	None but have found you imperfect—I only find no imperfection in you;
LG1867	2864	None but would subordinate you—I only am he who will never consent to subordinate you;
LG1867	2865	I only am he who places over you no master, owner,better, God, beyond what waits intrinsically in yourself.
LG1867	2866	Painters have painted their swarming groups, and the centre figure of all;
LG1867	2867	From the head of the centre figure spreading a nim-busnimbus of gold-color'd light;
LG1867	2868	But I paint myriads of heads, but paint no head with-outwithout its nimbus of gold-color'd light;
LG1867	2869	From my hand, from the brain of every man and woman it streams, effulgently flowing forever.
LG1867	2870	O I could sing such grandeurs and glories about you!
LG1867	2871	You have not known what you are—you have slum-berslumber'd upon yourself all your life;
LG1867	2872	Your eye-lids have been the same as closed most of the time;
LG1867	2873	What you have done returns already in mockeries;
LG1867	2874	(Your thrift, knowledge, prayers, if they do not return in mockeries, what is their return?)
LG1867	2875	The mockeries are not you;
LG1867	2876	Underneath them, and within them, I see you lurk;
LG1867	2877	I pursue you where none else has pursued you;
LG1867	2878	Silence, the desk, the flippant expression, the night,the accustom'd routine, if these conceal you from others, or from yourself, they do not conceal you from me;
LG1867	2879	The shaved face, the unsteady eye, the impure com-plexioncomplexion, if these balk others, they do not balk me,
LG1867	2880	The pert apparel, the deform'd attitude, drunkenness,greed, premature death, all these I part aside.
LG1867	2881	There is no endowment in man or woman that is not tallied in you;
LG1867	2882	There is no virtue, no beauty, in man or woman, but as good is in you;
LG1867	2883	No pluck, no endurance in others, but as good is in you;
LG1867	2884	No pleasure waiting for others, but an equal pleasure waits for you.
LG1867	2885	As for me, I give nothing to any one, except I give the like carefully to you;
LG1867	2886	I sing the songs of the glory of none, not God, sooner than I sing the songs of the glory of you.
LG1867	2887	Whoever you are! claim your own at any hazard!
LG1867	2888	These shows of the east and west are tame compared to you;
LG1867	2889	These immense meadows—these interminable rivers— you are immense and interminable as they;
LG1867	2890	These furies, elements, storms, motions of Nature,throes of apparent dissolution—you are he or she who is master or mistress over them,
LG1867	2891	Master or mistress in your own right over Nature,elements, pain, passion, dissolution.
LG1867	2892	The hopples fall from your ankles—you find an un-failingunfailing sufficiency;
LG1867	2893	Old or young, male or female, rude, low, rejected by the rest, whatever you are promulges itself;
LG1867	2894	Through birth, life, death, burial, the means are pro-videdprovided, nothing is scanted;
LG1867	2895	Through angers, losses, ambition, ignorance, ennui,what you are picks it way.
LG1867	2896	HOW they are provided for upon the earth, (appearing at intervals;)
LG1867	2897	How dear and dreadful they are to the earth;
LG1867	2898	How they inure to themselves as much as to any— What a paradox appears, their age;
LG1867	2899	How people respond to them, yet know them not;
LG1867	2900	How there is something relentless in their fate, all times;
LG1867	2901	How all times mischoose the objects of their adulation and reward,
LG1867	2902	And how the same inexorable price must still be paid for the same great purchase.
LG1867	2903	ALL submit to them, where they sit, inner, secure, unapproachable to analysis, in the Soul;
LG1867	2904	Not traditions—not the outer authorities are the judges—they are the judges of outer authori-tiesauthorities, and of all traditions;
LG1867	2905	They corroborate as they go, only whatever corrobo-ratescorroborates themselves, and touches themselves;
LG1867	2906	For all that, they have it forever in themselves to corroborate far and near, without one excep-tionexception.
LG1867	2907	ONLY themselves understand themselves, and the like of themselves,
LG1867	2908	As Souls only understand Souls.

LG1867	3016	The great city is that which has the greatest man or woman;

LG1867	3145	The shapes arise!
LG1867	3146	Shapes of doors giving many exits and entrances;
LG1867	3147	The door passing the dissever'd friend, flush'd and in haste;
LG1867	3148	The door that admits good news and bad news;
LG1867	3149	The door whence the son left home, confident and puff'd up;
LG1867	3150	The door he enter'd again from a long and scandalous absence, diseas'd, broken down, without inno-cenceinnocence, without means.
LG1867	3151	Her shape arises,
LG1867	3152	She, less guarded than ever, yet more guarded than ever;
LG1867	3153	The gross and soil'd she moves among do not make her gross and soil'd;
LG1867	3154	She knows the thoughts as she passes—nothing is con-cealconceal'd from her;
LG1867	3155	She is none the less considerate or friendly therefor;
LG1867	3156	She is the best-beloved—it is without exception—she has no reason to fear, and she does not fear;
LG1867	3157	Oaths, quarrels, hiccupp'd songs, smutty expressions,are idle to her as she passes;
LG1867	3158	She is silent—she is possess'd of herself—they do not offend her;
LG1867	3159	She receives them as the laws of nature receive them —she is strong,
LG1867	3160	She too is a law of nature—there is no law stronger than she is.
LG1867	3161	The main shapes arise!
LG1867	3162	Shapes of Democracy, total—result of centuries;
LG1867	3163	Shapes, ever projecting other shapes;
LG1867	3164	Shapes of a hundred Free States, begetting another hundred;
LG1867	3165	Shapes of turbulent manly cities;
LG1867	3166	Shapes of the women fit for These States,
LG1867	3167	Shapes of the friends and home-givers of the whole earth,
LG1867	3168	Shapes bracing the earth, and braced with the whole earth.
LG1867	3169	WITH antecedents;
LG1867	3170	With my fathers and mothers, and the accumulations of past ages;
LG1867	3171	With all which, had it not been, I would not now be here, as I am:

LG1867	3179	With the fading religions and priests;
LG1867	3180	With the small shores we look back to from our own large and present shores;

LG1867	3183	This year! sending itself ahead countless years to come.
LG1867	3184	O but it is not the years—it is I—it is You;
LG1867	3185	We touch all laws, and tally all antecedents;
LG1867	3186	We are the skald, the oracle, the monk, and the knight—we easily include them, and more;
LG1867	3187	We stand amid time, beginningless and endless—we stand amid evil and good;
LG1867	3188	All swings around us—there is as much darkness as light;
LG1867	3189	The very sun swings itself and its system of planets around us:
LG1867	3190	Its sun, and its again, all swing around us.
LG1867	3191	As for me, (torn, stormy, even as I, amid these ve-hementvehement days;)
LG1867	3192	I have the idea of all, and am all, and believe in all;
LG1867	3193	I believe materialism is true, and spiritualism is true— I reject no part.
LG1867	3194	Have I forgotten any part?
LG1867	3195	Come to me, whoever and whatever, till I give you recognition.
LG1867	3196	I respect Assyria, China, Teutonia, and the He-brewsHebrews;
LG1867	3197	I adopt each theory, myth, god, and demi-god;
LG1867	3198	I see that the old accounts, bibles, genealogies, are true, without exception;
LG1867	3199	I assert that all past days were what they should have been;
LG1867	3200	And that they could no-how have been better than they were,
LG1867	3201	And that to-day is what it should be—and that America is,
LG1867	3202	And that to-day and America could no-how be better than they are.
LG1867	3203	In the name of These States, and in your and my name, the Past,
LG1867	3204	And in the name of These States, and in your and my name, the Present time.
LG1867	3205	I know that the past was great, and the future will be great,

LG1867	3215	FLOOD-TIDE below me! I watch you face to face;

LG1867	3220	The impalpable sustenance of me from all things, at all hours of the day;

LG1867	3234	It avails not, neither time or place—distance avails not;
LG1867	3235	I am with you, you men and women of a generation, or ever so many generations hence;
LG1867	3236	I project myself—also I return—I am with you, and know how it is.
LG1867	3237	Just as you feel when you look on the river and sky so I felt;
LG1867	3238	Just as any of you is one of a living crowd, I was one of a crowd;
LG1867	3239	Just as you are refresh'd by the gladness of the river and the bright flow, I was refresh'd;

LG1867	3241	Just as you look on the numberless masts of ships, and the thick-stem'd pipes of steamboats, I look'd.
LG1867	3242	I too many and many a time cross'd the river, the sun half an hour high;
LG1867	3243	I watched the Twelfth-month sea-gulls—I saw them high in the air, floating with motionless wings, oscillating their bodies,

LG1867	3276	I too felt the curious abrupt questionings stir within me,

LG1867	3279	I too had been struck from the float forever held in solution;
LG1867	3280	I too had receiv'd identity by my Body;
LG1867	3281	That I was, I knew was of my body—and what I should be, I knew I should be of my body.
LG1867	3282	It is not upon you alone the dark patches fall,
LG1867	3283	The dark threw patches down upon me also;
LG1867	3284	The best I had done seem'd to me blank and sus-picioussuspicious;

LG1867	3286	It is not you alone who know what it is to be evil;
LG1867	3287	I am he who knew what it was to be evil;
LG1867	3288	I too knitted the old knot of contrariety,
LG1867	3289	Blabb'd, blush'd, resented, lied, stole, grudg'd,
LG1867	3290	Had guile, anger, lust, hot wishes I dared not speak,
LG1867	3291	Was wayward, vain, greedy, shallow, sly, cowardly, malignant,;

LG1867	3295	But I was Manhattanese, friendly and proud!
LG1867	3296	I was call'd by my nighest name by clear loud voices of young men as they saw me approaching or passing,
LG1867	3297	Felt their arms on my neck as I stood, or the negligent leaning of their flesh against me as I sat,

LG1867	3303	Closer yet I approach you;
LG1867	3304	What thought you have of me, I had as much of you —I laid in my stores in advance;
LG1867	3305	I consider'd long and seriously of you before you were born.
LG1867	3306	Who was to know what should come home to me?

LG1867	3314	Now I am curious what sight can ever be more stately and admirable to me than my mast-hemm'd Manhatta,

LG1867	3363	Keep on! Liberty is to be subserved, whatever occurs;

LG1867	3392	TO get betimes in Boston town, I rose this morning early;
LG1867	3393	Here's a good place at the corner—I must stand and see the show.

LG1867	3447	WHAT you give me, I cheerfully accept,
LG1867	3448	A little sustenance, a hut and garden, a little money— these, as I rendezvous with my poems,
LG1867	3449	A traveler's lodging and breakfast as I journey through The States—Why should I be ashamed to own such gifts? Why to advertise for them?
LG1867	3450	For I myself am not one who bestows nothing upon man and woman;
LG1867	3451	For I bestow upon any man or woman the entrance to all the gifts of the universe.
LG1867	3452	OUT of the rock'd cradle,

LG1867	3470	Throwing myself on the sand, confronting the waves,
LG1867	3471	I, chanter of pains and joys, uniter of here and hereafter,
LG1867	3472	Taking all hints to use them—but swiftly leaping beyond them,
LG1867	3473	A reminiscence sing.
LG1867	3474	Once, Paumanok,
LG1867	3475	When the snows had melted, and the Fifth-month grass was growing,
LG1867	3476	Up this sea-shore, in some briers,
LG1867	3477	Two guests from Alabama—two together,
LG1867	3478	And their nest, and four light-green eggs, spotted with brown,
LG1867	3479	And every day the he-bird, to and fro, near at hand,
LG1867	3480	And every day the she-bird, crouch'd on her nest,silent, with bright eyes,
LG1867	3481	And every day I, a curious boy, never too close, never disturbing them,
LG1867	3482	Cautiously peering, absorbing, translating.
LG1867	3483	Shine! shine! shine!
LG1867	3484	Pour down your warmth, great Sun!
LG1867	3485	While we bask—we two together.
LG1867	3486	Two together!
LG1867	3487	Winds blow South, or winds blow North,
LG1867	3488	Day come white, or night come black,
LG1867	3489	Home, or rivers and mountains from home,
LG1867	3490	Singing all time, minding no time,
LG1867	3491	If we two but keep together.
LG1867	3492	Till of a sudden,
LG1867	3493	May-be kill'd, unknown to her mate,
LG1867	3494	One forenoon the she-bird crouch'd not on the nest,
LG1867	3495	Nor return'd that afternoon, nor the next,
LG1867	3496	Nor ever appear'd again.
LG1867	3497	And thenceforward, all summer, in the sound of the sea,
LG1867	3498	And at night, under the full of the moon, in calmer weather,

LG1867	3500	Or flitting from brier to brier by day,
LG1867	3501	I saw, I heard at intervals, the remaining one, the he-bird,
LG1867	3502	The solitary guest from Alabama.
LG1867	3503	Blow! blow! blow!
LG1867	3504	Blow up, sea-winds, along Paumanok's shore!
LG1867	3505	I wait and I wait, till you blow my mate to me.
LG1867	3506	Yes, when the stars glisten'd,

LG1867	3511	He pour'd forth the meanings which I, of all men,know.
LG1867	3512	Yes, my brother, I know;
LG1867	3513	The rest might not—but I have treasur'd every note;
LG1867	3514	For once, and more than once, dimly, down to the beach gliding,

LG1867	3520	Listen'd, to keep, to sing—now translating the notes,

LG1867	3522	Soothe! soothe! soothe!
LG1867	3523	Close on its wave soothes the wave behind,
LG1867	3524	And again another behind, embracing and lapping, every one close,

LG1867	3527	O it is lagging—O I think it is heavy with love, with love.

LG1867	4109	Dancers at wedding-dances, kissers of brides, tender helpers of children, bearers of children,
LG1867	4110	Soldiers of revolts, standers by gaping graves, lower-erslowerers down of coffins,
LG1867	4111	Jouneyers over consecutive seasons, over the years— the curious years, each emerging from that which preceded it,
LG1867	4112	Journeyers as with companions, namely, their own diverse phases,
LG1867	4113	Forth-steppers from the latent unrealized baby-days,
LG1867	4114	Journeyers gayly with their own youth—Journeyers with their bearded and well-grain'd manhood,
LG1867	4115	Journeyers with their womanhood, ample, unsur-passunsurpass'd, content,
LG1867	4116	Journeyers with their own sublime old age of man-hoodmanhood or womanhood,
LG1867	4117	Old age, calm, expanded, broad with the haughty breadth of the universe,
LG1867	4118	Old age, flowing free with the delicious near-by free-domfreedom of death.
LG1867	4119	Allons! To that which is endless, as it was begin-ninglessbeginningless,
LG1867	4120	To undergo much, tramps of days, rests of nights,
LG1867	4121	To merge all in the travel they tend to, and the days and nights they tend to,
LG1867	4122	Again to merge them in the start of superior jour-neysjourneys;
LG1867	4123	To see nothing anywhere but what you may reach it and pass it,
LG1867	4124	To conceive no time, however distant, but what you may reach it and pass it,
LG1867	4125	To look up or down no road but it stretches and waits for you—however long, but it stretches and waits for you;
LG1867	4126	To see no being, not God's or any, but you also go thither,
LG1867	4127	To see no possession but you may possess it—enjoy-ingenjoying all without labor or purchase—abstracting the feast, yet not abstracting one particle of it;
LG1867	4128	To take the best of the farmer's farm and the rich man's elegant villa, and the chaste blessings of the well-married couple, and the fruits of or-chardsorchards and flowers of gardens,
LG1867	4129	To take to your use out of the compact cities as you pass through,
LG1867	4130	To carry buildings and streets with you afterward wherever you go,
LG1867	4131	To gather the minds of men out of their brains as you encounter them—to gather the love out of their hearts,
LG1867	4132	To take your lovers on the road with you, for all that you leave them behind you,
LG1867	4133	To know the universe itself as a road—as many roads —as roads for traveling souls.
LG1867	4134	The soul travels;
LG1867	4135	The body does not travel as much as the soul;
LG1867	4136	The body has just as great a work as the soul, and parts away at last for the journeys of the soul.
LG1867	4137	All parts away for the progress of souls;
LG1867	4138	All religion, all solid things, arts, governments,—all that was or is apparent upon this globe or any globe, falls into niches and corners before the procession of Souls along the grand roads of the universe.
LG1867	4139	Of the progress of the souls of men and women along the grand roads of the universe, all other progress is the needed emblem and sus-tenancesustenance.
LG1867	4140	Forever alive, forever forward,
LG1867	4141	Stately, solemn, sad, withdrawn, baffled, mad, turbu-lentturbulent, feeble, dissatisfied,
LG1867	4142	Desperate, proud, fond, sick, accepted by men, re-jectedrejected by men,
LG1867	4143	They go! they go! I know that they go, but I know not where they go;
LG1867	4144	But I know that they go toward the best—toward something great.
LG1867	4145	Allons! Whoever you are! come forth!
LG1867	4146	You must not stay sleeping and dallying there in the house, though you built it, or though it has been built for you.
LG1867	4147	Allons! out of the dark confinement!
LG1867	4148	It is useless to protest—I know all, and expose it.
LG1867	4149	Behold, through you as bad as the rest,
LG1867	4150	Through the laughter, dancing, dining, supping, of people,
LG1867	4151	Inside of dresses and ornaments, inside of those wash'd and trimm'd faces,
LG1867	4152	Behold a secret silent loathing and despair.
LG1867	4153	No husband, no wife, no friend, trusted to hear the confession;
LG1867	4154	Another self, a duplicate of every one, skulking and hiding it goes,
LG1867	4155	Formless and wordless through the streets of the cities, polite and bland in the parlors,
LG1867	4156	In the cars of rail-roads, in steamboats, in the public assembly,
LG1867	4157	Home to the houses of men and women, at the table,in the bed-room everywhere,
LG1867	4158	Smartly attired, countenance smiling, form upright,death under the breast-bones, hell under the skull-bones,
LG1867	4159	Under the broadcloth and gloves, under the ribbons and artificial flowers,
LG1867	4160	Keeping fair with the customs, speaking not a syllable of itself,
LG1867	4161	Speaking of anything else, but never of itself.
LG1867	4162	Allons! Through struggles and wars!
LG1867	4163	The goal that was named cannot be countermanded.
LG1867	4164	Have the past struggles succeeded?
LG1867	4165	What has succeeded? Yourself? Your nation? Na-tureNature?
LG1867	4166	Now understand me well—It is provided in the es-senceessence of things, that from any fruition of suc-cesssuccess, no matter what, shall come forth some-thingsomething to make a greater struggle necessary.
LG1867	4167	My call is the call of battle—I nourish active re-bellionrebellion?
LG1867	4168	He going with me must go well armed;
LG1867	4169	He going with me goes often with spare diet, poverty,angry enemies, desertions.
LG1867	4170	Allons! The road is before us!
LG1867	4171	It is safe—I have tried it—my own feet have tried it well.
LG1867	4172	Allons! Be not detain'd!
LG1867	4173	Let the paper remain on the desk unwritten, and the book on the shelf unopen'd!
LG1867	4174	Let the tools remain in the workshop! let the money remain unearn'd!
LG1867	4175	Let the school stand! mind not the cry of the teacher!
LG1867	4176	Let the preacher preach in the pulpit! let the lawyer plead in the court, and the judge expound the law.
LG1867	4177	Mon enfant! I give you my hand!
LG1867	4178	I give you my love, more precious than money,
LG1867	4179	I give you myself, before preaching or law;
LG1867	4180	Will you give me yourself? Will you come travel with me?
LG1867	4181	Shall we stick by each other as long as we live?
LG1867	4182	WHY reclining, interrogating? Why myself and all drowsing?
LG1867	4183	What deepening twilight! Scum floating atop of the waters!
LG1867	4184	Who are they, as bats and night-dogs, askant in the Capitol?
LG1867	4185	What a filthy Presidentiad! (O south, your torrid suns! O north, your arctic freezings!)
LG1867	4186	Judges? Is that the President?
LG1867	4187	Then I will sleep a while yet—for I see that These States sleep, for reasons;
LG1867	4188	(With gathering murk—with muttering thunder and lambent shoots, we all duly awake,
LG1867	4189	South, north, east, west, inland and seaboard, we will surely awake.)
LG1867	4190	HERE, take this gift!
LG1867	4191	I was reserving it for some hero, orator, or general,
LG1867	4192	One who should serve the good old cause, the great Idea, the progress and freedom of the race;
LG1867	4193	But I see that what I was reserving belongs to you just as much as to any.
LG1867	4194	COME closer to me;
LG1867	4195	Push close, my lovers, and take the best I possess;
LG1867	4196	Yield closer and closer, and give me the best you possess.
LG1867	4197	This is unfinish'd business with me—How is it with you?
LG1867	4198	(I was chill'd with the cold types, cylinder, wet paper between us.)
LG1867	4199	Male and Female!
LG1867	4200	I pass so poorly with paper and types, I must pass with the contact of bodies and souls.
LG1867	4201	American masses!
LG1867	4202	I do not thank you for liking me as I am, and liking the touch of me—I know that it is good for you to do so.
LG1867	4203	This is the poem of occupations;
LG1867	4204	In the labor of engines and trades, and the labor of fields, I find the developments,
LG1867	4205	And find the eternal meanings.
LG1867	4206	Workmen and Workwomen!
LG1867	4207	Were all educations, practical and ornamental, well display'd out of me, what would it amount to?
LG1867	4208	Were I as the head teacher, charitable proprietor,wise statesman, what would it amount to?
LG1867	4209	Were I to you as the boss employing and paying you,would that satisfy you?
LG1867	4210	The learn'd, virtuous, benevolent, and the usual terms;
LG1867	4211	A man like me, and never the usual terms.
LG1867	4212	Neither a servant nor a master am I;
LG1867	4213	I take no sooner a large price than a small price—I will have my own, whoever enjoys me;
LG1867	4214	I will be even with you, and you shall be even with me.
LG1867	4215	If you stand at work in a shop, I stand as nigh as the nighest in the same shop;
LG1867	4216	If you bestow gifts on your brother or dearest friend,I demand as good as your brother or dearest friend;
LG1867	4217	If your lover, husband, wife, is welcome by day or night, I must be personally as welcome;
LG1867	4218	If you become degraded, criminal, ill, then I become so for your sake;
LG1867	4219	If you remember your foolish and outlaw'd deeds, do you think I cannot remember my own foolish and outlaw'd deeds?
LG1867	4220	If you carouse at the table, I carouse at the opposite side of the table;
LG1867	4221	If you meet some stranger in the streets, and love him or her—why I often meet strangers in the street, and love them.
LG1867	4222	Why, what have you thought of yourself?
LG1867	4223	Is it you then that thought yourself less?
LG1867	4224	Is it you that thought the President greater than you?
LG1867	4225	Or the rich better off than you? or the educated wiser than you?
LG1867	4226	Because you are greasy or pimpled, or that you was once drunk, or a thief,
LG1867	4227	Or diseas'd, or rheumatic, or a prostitute, or are so now;
LG1867	4228	Or from frivolity or impotence, or that you are no scholar, and never saw your name in print,
LG1867	4229	Do you give in that you are any less immortal?
LG1867	4230	Souls of men and women! it is not you I call un-seenunseen, unheard, untouchable and untouching;
LG1867	4231	It is not you I go argue pro and con about, and to settle whether you are alive or no;
LG1867	4232	I own publicly who you are, if nobody else owns.
LG1867	4233	Grown, half-grown, and babe, of this country and every country, indoors and outdoors, one just as much as the other, I see,
LG1867	4234	And all else behind or through them.
LG1867	4235	The wife—and she is not one jot less than the husband;
LG1867	4236	The daughter—and she is just as good as the son;
LG1867	4237	The mother—and she is every bit as much as the father.
LG1867	4238	Offspring of ignorant and poor, boys apprenticed to trades,
LG1867	4239	Young fellows working on farms, and old fellows working on farms,
LG1867	4240	Sailor-men, merchant-men, coasters, immigrants,
LG1867	4241	All these I see—but nigher and farther the same I see;
LG1867	4242	None shall escape me, and none shall wish to escape me.
LG1867	4243	I bring what you much need, yet always have,
LG1867	4244	Not money, amours, dress, eating, but as good;
LG1867	4245	I send no agent or medium, offer no representative of value, but offer the value itself.
LG1867	4246	There is something that comes home to one now and perpetually;
LG1867	4247	It is not what is printed, preach'd, discuss'd—it eludes discussion and print;
LG1867	4248	It is not to be put in a book—it is not in this book;
LG1867	4249	It is for you, whoever you are—it is no farther from you than your hearing and sight are from you;
LG1867	4250	It is hinted by nearest, commonest; readiest—it is ever provoked by them.
LG1867	4251	You may read in many languages, yet read nothing about it;
LG1867	4252	You may read the President's Message, and read nothing about it there;
LG1867	4253	Nothing in the reports from the State department or Treasury department, or in the daily papers or the weekly papers,
LG1867	4254	Or in the census or revenue returns, prices current,or any accounts of stock.
LG1867	4255	The sun and stars that float in the open air;
LG1867	4256	The apple-shaped earth, and we upon it—surely the drift of them is something grand!
LG1867	4257	I do not know what it is, except that it is grand, and that it is happiness,
LG1867	4258	And that the enclosing purport of us here is not a speculation, or bon-mot, or reconnoissance,
LG1867	4259	And that it is not something which by luck may turn out well for us, and without luck must be a failure for us,
LG1867	4260	And not something which may yet be retracted in a certain contingency.
LG1867	4261	The light and shade, the curious sense of body and identity, the greed that with perfect complais-ancecomplaisance devours all things, the endless pride and out-stretching of man, unspeakable joys and sorrows,
LG1867	4262	The wonder every one sees in every one else he sees,and the wonders that fill each minute of time forever,
LG1867	4263	What have you reckon'd them for, camerado?
LG1867	4264	Have you reckon'd them for a trade, or farm-work?or for the profits of a store?
LG1867	4265	Or to achieve yourself a position? or to fill a gentle-mangentleman's leisure, or a lady's leisure?
LG1867	4266	Have you reckon'd the landscape took substance and form that it might be painted in a picture?
LG1867	4267	Or men and women that they might be written of,and songs sung?
LG1867	4268	Or the attraction of gravity, and the great laws and harmonious combinations, and the fluids of the air, as subjects for the savans?
LG1867	4269	Or the brown land and the blue sea for maps and charts?
LG1867	4270	Or the stars to be put in constellations and named fancy names?
LG1867	4271	Or that the growth of seeds is for agricultural tables,or agriculture itself?
LG1867	4272	Old institutions—these arts, libraries, legends, col-lectionscollections, and the practice handed along in man-ufacturesmanufactures—will we rate them so high?
LG1867	4273	Will we rate our cash and business high ?—I have no objection;
LG1867	4274	I rate them as high as the highest—then a child born of a woman and man I rate beyond all rate.
LG1867	4275	We thought our Union grand, and our Constitution grand;
LG1867	4276	I do not say they are not grand and good, for they are;
LG1867	4277	I am this day just as much in love with them as you;
LG1867	4278	Then I am in love with you, and with all my fellows upon the earth.
LG1867	4279	We consider bibles and religions divine—I do not say they are not divine;
LG1867	4280	I say they have all grown out of you, and may grow out of you still;
LG1867	4281	It is not they who give the life—it is you who give the life;
LG1867	4282	Leaves are not more shed from the trees, or trees from the earth, than they are shed out of you.
LG1867	4283	When the psalm sings instead of the singer;
LG1867	4284	When the script preaches, instead of the preacher;
LG1867	4285	When the pulpit descends and goes, instead of the carver that carved the supporting desk;
LG1867	4286	When I can touch the body of books, by night or by day, and when they touch my body back again;
LG1867	4287	When a university course convinces, like a slumber-ingslumbering woman and child convince;
LG1867	4288	When the minted gold in the vault smiles like the night-watchman's daughter;
LG1867	4289	When warrantee deeds loafe in chairs opposite, and are my friendly companions;
LG1867	4290	I intend to reach them my hand, and make as much of them as I do of men and women like you.
LG1867	4291	The sum of all known reverence I add up in you,whoever you are;
LG1867	4292	The President is there in the White House for you— it is not you who are here for him;
LG1867	4293	The Secretaries act in their bureaus for you—not you here for them;
LG1867	4294	The Congress convenes every Twelfth-month for you;
LG1867	4295	Laws, courts, the forming of States, the charters of cities, the going and coming of commerce and mails, are all for you.
LG1867	4296	List close, my scholars dear!
LG1867	4297	All doctrines, all politics and civilization, exurge from you;
LG1867	4298	All sculpture and monuments, and anything inscribed anywhere, are tallied in you;
LG1867	4299	The gist of histories and statistics as far back as the records reach, is in you this hour, and myths and tales the same;
LG1867	4300	If you were not breathing and walking here, where would they all be?
LG1867	4301	The most renown'd poems would be ashes, orations and plays would be vacuums.
LG1867	4302	All architecture is what you do to it when you look upon it;
LG1867	4303	(Did you think it was in the white or gray stone? or the lines of the arches and cornices?)
LG1867	4304	All music is what awakes from you, when you are reminded by the instruments;
LG1867	4305	It is not the violins and the cornets—it is not the oboe nor the beating drums, nor the score of the baritone singer singing his sweet romanza—nor that of the men's chorus, nor that of the wo-menwomen's chorus,
LG1867	4306	It is nearer and farther than they.
LG1867	4307	Will the whole come back then?
LG1867	4308	Can each see signs of the best by a look in the look-inglooking-glass-glass? is there nothing greater or more?
LG1867	4309	Does all sit there with you, with the mystic, unseen Soul?
LG1867	4310	Strange and hard that paradox true I give;
LG1867	4311	Objects gross and the unseen Soul are one.
LG1867	4312	House-building, measuring, sawing the boards;
LG1867	4313	Blacksmithing, glass-blowing, nail-making, coopering,tin-rooting, shingle-dressing,

LG1867	4335	The daily routine of your own or any man's life—the shop, yard, store, or factory;
LG1867	4336	These shows all near you by day and night—work-menworkmen! whoever you are, your daily life!
LG1867	4337	In that and them the heft of the heaviest—in them far more than you estimated, and far less also;
LG1867	4338	In them realities for you and me—in them poems for you and me;
LG1867	4339	In them, not yourself—you and your Soul enclose all things, regardless of estimation;
LG1867	4340	In them the development good—in them, all themes and hints.
LG1867	4341	I do not affirm what you see beyond is futile—I do not advise you to stop;
LG1867	4342	I do not say leadings you thought great are not great;
LG1867	4343	But I say that none lead to greater, than those lead to.
LG1867	4344	Will you seek afar off? You surely come back at last,
LG1867	4345	In things best known to you, finding the best, or as good as the best,
LG1867	4346	In folks nearest to you finding the sweetest, strongest,lovingest;

LG1867	4348	Man in the first you see or touch—always in friend,brother, nighest neighbor—Woman in mother,lover, wife;
LG1867	4349	The popular tastes and employments taking precedence in poems or any where,
LG1867	4350	You workwomen and workmen of these States having your own divine and strong life,
LG1867	4351	And all else giving place to men and women like you.
LG1867	4352	HE is wisest who has the most caution;
LG1867	4353	He only wins who goes far enough.
LG1867	4354	ANY thing is as good as established, when that is estab-lishedestablished that will produce it and continue it.
LG1867	4355	O HASTENING light!
LG1867	4356	O free and extatic! O what I here, preparing, warble for!
LG1867	4357	O the sun of the world will ascend, dazzling, and take his height—and you too will ascend!
LG1867	4358	O so amazing and broad—up there resplendent, dart-ingdarting and burning!
LG1867	4359	O vision prophetic, stagger'd with weight of light! with pouring glories!
LG1867	4360	O lips of my soul, already becoming powerless!
LG1867	4361	O ample and grand Presidentiads!
LG1867	4362	New history! new heroes! I project you!
LG1867	4363	Visions of poets! only you really last! O sweep on! sweep on!
LG1867	4364	O heights too swift and dizzy yet!
LG1867	4365	O purged and luminous! you threaten me more than I can stand!
LG1867	4366	(I must not venture—the ground under my feet men-acesmenaces me—it will not support me;)

LG1867	4368	TEARS! tears! tears!
LG1867	4369	In the night, in solitude, tears;
LG1867	4370	On the white shore dripping, dripping, suck'd in by the sand;
LG1867	4371	Tears—not a star shining—all dark and desolate;
LG1867	4372	Moist tears from the eyes of a muffled head:
LG1867	4373	—O who is that ghost?—that form in the dark, with tears?
LG1867	4374	What shapeless lump is that, bent, crouch'd there on the sand?
LG1867	4375	Streaming tears—sobbing tears—throes, choked with wild cries;
LG1867	4376	O storm, embodied, rising, careering, with swift steps along the beach;
LG1867	4377	O wild and dismal night storm, with wind! O belch-ingbelching and desperate!
LG1867	4378	O shade, so sedate and decorous by day, with calm countenance and regulated pace;
LG1867	4379	But away, at night, as you fly, none looking—O then the unloosen'd ocean,
LG1867	4380	Of tears! tears! tears!
LG1867	4381	ABOARD, at the ship's helm,
LG1867	4382	A young steersman, steering with care.
LG1867	4383	A bell through fog on a sea-coast dolefully ringing,
LG1867	4384	An ocean-bell—O a warning bell, rock'd by the waves.
LG1867	4385	O you give good notice indeed, you bell by the sea-reefssea-reefs ringing,
LG1867	4386	Ringing, ringing, to warn the ship from its wreck-placewreck-place.
LG1867	4387	For, as on the alert, O steersman, you mind the bell's admonition,

LG1867	4390	But O the ship, the immortal ship! O ship aboard the ship!
LG1867	4391	O ship of the body—ship of the soul—voyaging, voy-agingvoyaging, voyaging.
LG1867	4392	AMERICA always!
LG1867	4393	Always our own feuillage!

LG1867	4396	Always the vast slope drain'd by the Southern Sea— inseparable with the slopes drain'd by the Eastern and Western Seas;

LG1867	4460	Southward there, I screaming, with wings slow flap-pingflapping, with the myriads of gulls wintering along the coasts of Florida—or in Louisiana, with pelicans breeding,

LG1867	4462	Northward, on the sands, on some shallow bay of Pau-manokPaumanok, I, with parties of snowy herons wading in the wet to seek worms and aquatic plants;

LG1867	4464	The migrating flock of wild geese alighting in autumn to refresh themselves—the body of the flock feed—the sentinels outside move around with erect heads watching, and are from time to time reliev'd by other sentinels—And I feeding and taking turns with the rest;

LG1867	4466	In the Mannahatta, streets, piers, shipping, store-housesstorehouses, and the countless workmen working in the shops,

LG1867	4469	Nativities, climates, the grass of the great Pastoral Plains,
LG1867	4470	Cities, labors, death, animals, products, good and evil —these me,
LG1867	4471	These affording, in all their particulars, endless feuillage to me and to America, how can I do less than pass the clew of the union of them, to afford the like to you?
LG1867	4472	Whoever you are! how can I but offer you divine leaves, that you also be eligible as I am?
LG1867	4473	How can I but, as here, chanting, invite you for your-selfyourself to collect bouquets of the incomparable feuillage of These States?
LG1867	4474	I WAS asking for something specific and perfect for my city,
LG1867	4475	Whereupon, lo! upsprang the aboriginal name!
LG1867	4476	Now I see what there is in a name, a word, liquid, sane, unruly, musical, self-sufficient;
LG1867	4477	I see that the word of my city is that word up there,
LG1867	4478	Because I see that word nested in nests of water-bays, superb, with tall and wonderful spires,
LG1867	4479	Rich, hemm'd thick all around with sailships and steamships—an island sixteen miles long, solid-foundedsolid-founded,
LG1867	4480	Numberless crowded streets—high growths of iron, slender, strong, light, splendidly uprising to-wardtoward clear skies;
LG1867	4481	Tides swift and ample, well-loved by me, toward sun-downsundown,

LG1867	4492	A million people—manners free and superb—open voices—hospitality—the most courageous and friendly young men;
LG1867	4493	The free city! no slaves! no owners of slaves!
LG1867	4494	The beautiful city, the city of hurried and sparkling waters! the city of spires and masts!
LG1867	4495	The city nested in bays! my city!
LG1867	4496	The city of such women, I am mad to be with them! I will return after death to be with them!
LG1867	4497	The city of such young men, I swear I cannot live happy, without I often go talk, walk, eat, drink, sleep, with them!
LG1867	4498	LET us twain walk aside from the rest;
LG1867	4499	Now we are together privately, do you discard cere-monyceremony;
LG1867	4500	Come! vouchsafe to me what has yet been vouch-safedvouchsafed to none—Tell me the whole story,
LG1867	4501	Tell me what you would not tell your brother, wife, husband, or physician.
LG1867	4502	A GREAT year and place;
LG1867	4503	A harsh, discordant, natal scream out-sounding, to touch the mother's heart closer than any yet.
LG1867	4504	I walk'd the shores of my Eastern Sea,
LG1867	4505	Heard over the waves the little voice,
LG1867	4506	Saw the divine infant, where she woke, mournfully wailing, amid the roar of cannon, curses, shouts, crash of falling buildings;
LG1867	4507	Was not so sick from the blood in the gutters running —nor from the single corpses, nor those in heaps, nor those borne away in the tumbrils;
LG1867	4508	Was not so desperate at the battues of death—was not so shock'd at the repeated fusillades of the guns.
LG1867	4509	Pale, silent, stern, what could I say to that long-accruedlong-accrued retribution?
LG1867	4510	Could I wish humanity different?
LG1867	4511	Could I wish the people made of wood and stone?
LG1867	4512	Or that there be no justice in destiny or time?
LG1867	4513	O Liberty! O mate for me!
LG1867	4514	Here too the blaze, the bullet and the axe, in reserve, to fetch them out in case of need;
LG1867	4515	Here too, though long represt, can never be destroy'd;
LG1867	4516	Here too could rise at last, murdering and extatic;
LG1867	4517	Here too demanding full arrears of vengeance.
LG1867	4518	Hence I sign this salute over the sea,
LG1867	4519	And I do not deny that terrible red birth and baptism,
LG1867	4520	But remember the little voice that I heard wailing— and wait with perfect trust, no matter how long;
LG1867	4521	And from to-day, sad and cogent, I maintain the be-queathbequeath'd cause, as for all lands,
LG1867	4522	And I send these words to Paris with my love,
LG1867	4523	And I guess some chansonniers there will understand them,
LG1867	4524	For I guess there is latent music yet in France— floods of it;
LG1867	4525	O I hear already the bustle of instruments—they will soon be drowning all that would interrupt them;
LG1867	4526	O I think the east wind brings a triumphal and free march,
LG1867	4527	It reaches hither—it swells me to joyful madness,
LG1867	4528	I will run transpose it in words, to justify it,
LG1867	4529	I will yet sing a song for you, ma femme.
LG1867	4530	HOLD it up sternly! See this it sends back! (Who is it? Is it you?)
LG1867	4531	Outside fair costume—within ashes and filth,
LG1867	4532	No more a flashing eye—no more a sonorous voice or springy step;
LG1867	4533	Now some slave's eye, voice, hands, step,
LG1867	4534	A drunkard's breath, unwholesome eater's face, venerealee's flesh,
LG1867	4535	Lungs rotting away piecemeal, stomach sour and can-kerouscankerous,
LG1867	4536	Joints rheumatic, bowels clogged with abomination,
LG1867	4537	Blood circulating dark and poisonous streams,
LG1867	4538	Words babble, hearing and touch callous,
LG1867	4539	No brain, no heart left—no magnetism of sex;
LG1867	4540	Such, from one look in this looking-glass ere you go hence,
LG1867	4541	Such a result so soon—and from such a beginning!
LG1867	4542	OF the visages of things—And of piercing through to the accepted hells beneath;
LG1867	4543	Of ugliness—To me there is just as much in it as there is in beauty—And now the ugliness of human beings is acceptable to me;
LG1867	4544	Of detected persons—To me, detected persons are not, in any respect, worse than undetected per-sonspersons—and are not in any respect worse than I am myself;
LG1867	4545	Of criminals—To me, any judge, or any juror, is equally criminal—and any reputable person is also—and the President is also.
LG1867	4546	OF waters, forests, hills;
LG1867	4547	Of the earth at large, whispering through medium of me;
LG1867	4548	Of vista—Suppose some sight in arriere, through the formative chaos, presuming the growth, fulness, life, now attain'd on the journey;
LG1867	4549	(But I see the road continued, and the journey ever continued;)
LG1867	4550	Of what was once lacking on earth, and in due time has become supplied—And of what will yet be supplied,
LG1867	4551	Because all I see and know, I believe to have purport in what will yet be supplied.
LG1867	4552	OF persons arrived at high positions, ceremonies, wealth, scholarships, and the like;
LG1867	4553	To me, all that those persons have arrived at, sinks away from them, except as it results to their Bodies and Souls,
LG1867	4554	So that often to me they appear gaunt and naked;
LG1867	4555	And often, to me, each one mocks the others, and mocks himself or herself,
LG1867	4556	And of each one, the core of life, namely happiness, is full of the rotten excrement of maggots,
LG1867	4557	And often, to me, those men and women pass unwit-tinglyunwittingly the true realities of life, and go toward false realities,
LG1867	4558	And often, to me, they are alive after what custom has served them, but nothing more,
LG1867	4559	And often, to me, they are sad, hasty, unwaked son- nambules, walking the dusk.
LG1867	4560	OF ownership—As if one fit to own things could not at pleasure enter upon all, and incorporate them into himself or herself;
LG1867	4561	Of Equality—As if it harm'd me, giving others the same chances and rights as myself—As if it were not indispensable to my own rights that others possess the same;
LG1867	4562	Of Justice—As if Justice could be anything but the same ample law, expounded by natural judges and saviors,
LG1867	4563	As if it might be this thing or that thing, according to decisions.
LG1867	4564	AS I sit with others, at a great feast, suddenly, while the music is playing,
LG1867	4565	To my mind, (whence it comes I know not,) spectral, in mist, of a wreck at sea,
LG1867	4566	Of the flower of the marine science of fifty generations, founder'd off the Northeast coast, and going down—Of the steamship Arctic going down,
LG1867	4567	Of the veil'd tableau—Women gather'd together on deck, pale, heroic, waiting the moment that draws so close—O the moment!
LG1867	4568	O the huge sob—A few bubbles—the white foam spirting up—And then the women gone,
LG1867	4569	Sinking there, while the passionless wet flows on— And I now pondering, Are those women indeed gone?
LG1867	4570	Are Souls drown'd and destroy'd so?
LG1867	4571	Is only matter triumphant?
LG1867	4572	OF what I write from myself—As if that were not the resumé;
LG1867	4573	Of Histories—As if such, however complete, were not less complete than my poems;
LG1867	4574	As if the shreds, the records of nations, could possibly be as lasting as my poems;
LG1867	4575	As if here were not the amount of all nations, and of all the lives of heroes.
LG1867	4576	OF obedience, faith, adhesiveness;
LG1867	4577	As I stand aloof and look, there is to me something profoundly affecting in large masses of men, following the lead of those who do not believe in men.
LG1867	4578	MY spirit to yours, dear brother;
LG1867	4579	Do not mind because many, sounding your name, do not understand you;
LG1867	4580	I do not sound your name, but I understand you, (there are others also;)
LG1867	4581	I specify you with joy, O my comrade, to salute you, and to salute those who are with you, before and since—and those to come also,
LG1867	4582	That we all labor together, transmitting the same charge and succession;
LG1867	4583	We few, equals, indifferent of lands, indifferent of times;
LG1867	4584	We, enclosers of all continents, all castes—allowers of all theologies,
LG1867	4585	Compassionaters, perceivers, rapport of men,
LG1867	4586	We walk silent among disputes and assertions, but reject not the disputers, nor any thing that is asserted;
LG1867	4587	We hear the bawling and din—we are reach'd at by divisions, jealousies, recriminations on every side,
LG1867	4588	They close peremptorily upon us, to surround us, my comrade,

LG1867	4590	Till we saturate time and eras, that the men and women of races, ages to come, may prove brethren and lovers, as we are.
LG1867	4591	I SEE in you the estuary that enlarges and spreads itself grandly as it pours in the great Sea.
LG1867	4592	FROM all the rest I single out you, having a message for you:
LG1867	4593	You are to die—Let others tell you what they please, I cannot prevaricate,
LG1867	4594	I am exact and merciless, but I love you—There is no escape for you.
LG1867	4595	Softly I lay my right hand upon you—you just feel it,
LG1867	4596	I do not argue—I bend my head close, and half-envelophalf-envelop it,
LG1867	4597	I sit quietly by—I remain faithful,
LG1867	4598	I am more than nurse, more than parent or neighbor,
LG1867	4599	I absolve you from all except yourself, spiritual, bodily —that is eternal,
LG1867	4600	(The corpse you will leave will be but excrementitious.)
LG1867	4601	The sun bursts through in unlooked-for directions!
LG1867	4602	Strong thoughts fill you, and confidence—you smile!
LG1867	4603	You forget you are sick, as I forget you are sick,
LG1867	4604	You do not see the medicines—you do not mind the weeping friends—I am with you,
LG1867	4605	I exclude others from you—there is nothing to be commiserated,
LG1867	4606	I do not commiserate—I congratulate you.
LG1867	4607	STRANGER! if you, passing, meet me, and desire to speak to me, why should you not speak to me?
LG1867	4608	And why should I not speak to you?
LG1867	4609	NATIONS ten thousand years before These States, and many times ten thousand years before These States;
LG1867	4610	Garner'd clusters of ages, that men and women like us grew up and travel'd their course, and pass'd on;
LG1867	4611	What vast-built cities—what orderly republics—what pastoral tribes and nomads;
LG1867	4612	What histories, rulers, heroes, perhaps trancendingtranscending all others;
LG1867	4613	What laws, customs, wealth, arts, traditions;
LG1867	4614	What sort of marriage—what costumes—what phy-siologyphysiology and phrenology;
LG1867	4615	What of liberty and slavery among them—what they thought of death and the soul;
LG1867	4616	Who were witty and wise—who beautiful and poetic— who brutish and undevelop'd;
LG1867	4617	Not a mark, not a record remains—And yet all re-mainsremains.
LG1867	4618	O I know that those men and women were not for nothing, any more than we are for nothing;
LG1867	4619	I know that they belong to the scheme of the world every bit as much as we now belong to it, and as all will henceforth belong to it.
LG1867	4620	Afar they stand—yet near to me they stand,
LG1867	4621	Some with oval countenances, learn'd and calm,
LG1867	4622	Some naked and savage—Some like huge collections of insects,
LG1867	4623	Some in tents—herdsmen, patriarchs, tribes, horse-menhorsemen,

LG1867	4625	Some traversing paved avenues, amid temples, pala-cespalaces, factories, libraries, shows, courts, theatres, wonderful monuments.
LG1867	4626	Are those billions of men really gone?
LG1867	4627	Are those women of the old experience of the earth gone?
LG1867	4628	Do their lives, cities, arts, rest only with us?
LG1867	4629	Did they achieve nothing for good, for themselves?
LG1867	4630	I believe of all those billions of men and women that fill'd the unnamed lands, every one exists this hour, here or elsewhere, invisible to us, in exact proportion to what he or she grew from in life, and out of what he or she did, felt, be-camebecame, loved, sinned, in life.
LG1867	4631	I believe that was not the end of those nations, or any person of them, any more than this shall be the end of my nation, or of me;
LG1867	4632	Of their languages, governments, marriage, literature, products, games, wars, manners, crimes, pris-onsprisons, slaves, heroes, poets, I suspect their re-sultsresults curiously await in the yet unseen world— counterparts of what accrued to them in the seen world,
LG1867	4633	I suspect I shall meet them there,
LG1867	4634	I suspect I shall there find each old particular of those unnamed lands.
LG1867	4635	WHO includes diversity, and is Nature,
LG1867	4636	Who is the amplitude of the earth, and the coarseness and sexuality of the earth, and the great char-itycharity of the earth, and the equilibrium also,
LG1867	4637	Who has not look'd forth from the windows, the eyes, for nothing, or whose brain held audience with messengers for nothing;
LG1867	4638	Who contains believers and disbelievers—Who is the most majestic lover;
LG1867	4639	Who holds duly his or her triune proportion of real-ismrealism, spiritualism, and of the æsthetic, or in-tellectualintelltual,

LG1867	4641	Who, out of the theory of the earth, and of his or her body, understands by subtle analogies all other theories,
LG1867	4642	The theory of a city, a poem, and of the large poli-ticspolitics of These States;
LG1867	4643	Who believes not only in our globe, with its sun and moon, but in other globes, with their suns and moons;
LG1867	4644	Who, constructing the house of himself or herself, not for a day, but for all time, sees races, eras, dates, generations,
LG1867	4645	The past, the future, dwelling there, like space, insep-arableinseparable together.
LG1867	4646	WHEN I read the book, the biography famous;
LG1867	4647	And is this, then, (said I,) what the author calls a man's life?
LG1867	4648	And so will some one, when I am dead and gone, write my life?
LG1867	4649	(As if any man really knew aught of my life;
LG1867	4650	As if you, O cunning Soul, did not keep your secret well!)
LG1867	4651	I SAY whatever tastes sweet to the most perfect person —That is finally right.
LG1867	4652	I SAY the human shape or face is so great, it must never be made ridiculous;
LG1867	4653	I say for ornaments nothing outré can be allowed,
LG1867	4654	And that anything is most beautiful without orna-mentornament,
LG1867	4655	And that exaggerations will be sternly revenged in your own physiology; and in other persons' phys-iologyphysiology also;
LG1867	4656	And I say that clean-shaped children can be jetted and conceiv'd only where natural forms prevail in public, and the human face and form are never caricatured;
LG1867	4657	And I say that genius need never more be turn'd to romances,
LG1867	4658	(For facts properly told, how mean appear all ro-mancesromances.)
LG1867	4659	I HAVE said many times that materials and the Soul are great, and that all depends on physique;
LG1867	4660	Now I reverse what I said, and affirm that all depends on the æsthetic, or intellectual,
LG1867	4661	And that criticism is great—and that refinement is greatest of all;
LG1867	4662	And I affirm now that the mind governs—and that all depends on the mind.
LG1867	4663	WITH one man or woman—(no matter which one—I even pick out the lowest,)
LG1867	4664	With him or her I now illustrate the whole law;
LG1867	4665	I say that every right, in politics or what-not, shall be eligible to that one man or woman, on the same terms as any.
LG1867	4666	DESPAIRING cries float ceaselessly toward me, day and night,
LG1867	4667	The sad voice of Death—the call of my nearest lover, putting forth, alarm'd, uncertain,
LG1867	4668	The Sea I am quickly to sail, come tell me,
LG1867	4669	Come tell me where I am speeding—tell me my destina-tiondestination.
LG1867	4670	I understand your anguish, but I cannot help you,
LG1867	4671	I approach, hear, behold—the sad mouth, the look out of the eyes, your mute inquiry,
LG1867	4672	Whither I go from the bed I recline on, come tell me:
LG1867	4673	Old age, alarm'd, uncertain—A young woman's voice, appealing to me for comfort;
LG1867	4674	A young man's voice, Shall I not escape?
LG1867	4675	A THOUSAND perfect men and women appear,
LG1867	4676	Around each gathers a cluster of friends, and gay children and youths, with offerings.
LG1867	4677	O TO make the most jubilant poems!
LG1867	4678	O full of music! Full of manhood, womanhood, in-fancyinfancy!

LG1867	4682	O for the sunshine, and motion of waves in a poem.

LG1867	4684	O to sail in a ship under full sail at sea.

LG1867	4686	It is not enough to have this globe, or a certain time —I will have thousands of globes, and all time.

LG1867	4694	I hear the alarm at dead of night,
LG1867	4695	I hear bells—shouts!—I pass the crowd—I run!
LG1867	4696	The sight of the flames maddens me with pleasure.

LG1867	4714	O to have been brought up on bays, lagoons, creeks or along the coast!
LG1867	4715	O to continue and be employ'd there all my life!
LG1867	4716	O the briny and damp smell—the shore—the salt weeds exposed at low water,

LG1867	4718	O it is I!
LG1867	4719	I come with my clam-rake and spade! I come with my eel-spear;
LG1867	4720	Is the tide out? I join the group of clam-diggers on the flats,
LG1867	4721	I laugh and work with them—I joke at my work, like a mettlesome young man.
LG1867	4722	In winter I take my eel-basket and eel-spear and travel out on foot on the ice—I have a small axe to cut holes in the ice;
LG1867	4723	Behold me, well-clothed, going gaily, or returning in the afternoon—my brood of tough boys accom-panyingaccompanying me,
LG1867	4724	My brood of grown and part-grown boys, who love to be with no one else so well as they love to be with me,
LG1867	4725	By day to work with me, and by night to sleep with me.
LG1867	4726	Or, another time, in warm weather, out in a boat,to lift the lobster-pots, where they are sunk with heavy stones, (I know the buoys;)
LG1867	4727	O the sweetness of the Fifth-month morning upon the water, as I row, just before sunrise, toward the buoys;
LG1867	4728	I pull the wicker pots up slantingly—the dark green lobsters are desperate with their claws, as I take them out—I insert wooden pegs in the joints of their pincers,
LG1867	4729	I go to all the places, one after another, and then row back to the shore,
LG1867	4730	There, in a huge kettle of boiling water, the lobsters shall be boil'd till their color becomes scarlet.
LG1867	4731	Or, another time, mackerel-taking,
LG1867	4732	Voracious, mad for the hook, near the surface, they seem to fill the water for miles;
LG1867	4733	Or, another time, fishing for rock-fish in Chesapeake Bay—I one of the brown-faced crew;
LG1867	4734	Or, another time, trailing for blue-fish off Paumanok,I stand with braced body,

LG1867	4736	In sight around me the quick veering and darting of fifty skiffs, my companions.

LG1867	4769	O the old manhood of me, my joy!

LG1867	4835	RESPONDEZ! Respondez!
LG1867	4836	Let every one answer! let those who sleep be waked! let none evade!
LG1867	4837	(How much longer must we go on with our affectations and sneaking?
LG1867	4838	Let me bring this to a close—I pronounce openly for a new distribution of roles;)
LG1867	4839	Let that which stood in front go behind! and let that which was behind advance to the front and speak;

LG1867	4894	BY the City Dead-House, by the gate,

LG1867	4911	WHAT General has a good army in himself, has a good army;
LG1867	4912	He happy in himself, or she happy in herself, is happy.
LG1867	4913	THINK of the Soul;
LG1867	4914	I swear to you that body of yours gives proportions to your Soul somehow to live in other spheres;
LG1867	4915	I do not know how, but I know it is so.
LG1867	4916	Think of loving and being loved;
LG1867	4917	I swear to you, whoever you are, you can interfuse yourself with such things that everybody that sees you shall look longingly upon you.
LG1867	4918	Think of the past;
LG1867	4919	I warn you that in a little while, others will find their past in you and your times.
LG1867	4920	The race is never separated—nor man nor woman escapes;

LG1867	4934	Have I not said that womanhood involves all?
LG1867	4935	Have I not told how the universe has nothing better than the best womanhood?
LG1867	4936	UNFOLDED out of the folds of the woman, man comes unfolded, and is always to come unfolded;
